Now I'm looking at merging the two Discussion sentences: discriminability doesn't guarantee filtering utility—on arXiv with K=3, graduated filtering still underperforms binary—and the LightGBM AUC decline across scales suggests the trend may continue at larger parameters.

For the Conclusion, I need to compress the findings about K values across domains and model scales into plain language. The key insight is that K varies by domain and model capacity, with a two-tier ceiling emerging, and cross-model transfer preserves most accuracy despite boundary-level variation.

The next thinking mentions that K remains fragile at boundaries and outlines future work on retrain-chain measurement and scaling beyond 32B. I'll fold this into a concise wrap-up about the limitations and next steps.

Under prompt-chain recursion at θ=0.60, K equals 2 for web text across most configurations, 1–2 for code, and 3 for arXiv, with this domain ordering holding regardless of θ. Fine-tuned DeBERTa hits a ceiling at K=2 on C4 but extends to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv; cross-model transfer preserves 80–92% accuracy overall, though K estimates remain unstable at domain boundaries. Future work should measure K under retrain-chain conditions and explore scaling beyond 32B.

    "oldString": "Discriminability ($\\Kempirical$) does not automatically translate to filtering utility: on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering still fails to outperform binary (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), suggesting that depth scores beyond the first boundary carry insufficient signal for training data selection.\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.618 (32B) across three scales (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); whether this trend continues at 70B+ parameters warrants investigation.",
    "newString": "Discriminability ($\\Kempirical$) does not automatically translate to filtering utility: on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering still fails to outperform binary (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.618 (32B); extrapolation to 70B+ warrants investigation.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nEven frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \\geq 2$ ranges 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix).\nSince all main results use prompt-chain (fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain), $\\Kempirical{=}2$ establishes a detection floor; retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) represents the upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$), while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); the retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) extends $\\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nThe domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), and the classifier gap scales accordingly: DeBERTa recovers $+0$ on C4, $+1$ on Wikipedia, and $+1$ on arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), with the zero gap on C4 (stable across 1.5B--32B) indicating fundamental information loss rather than classifier limitation.\nDiscriminability ($\\Kempirical$) does not automatically translate to filtering utility: on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering still fails to outperform binary (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), suggesting that depth scores beyond the first boundary carry insufficient signal for training data selection.\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.618 (32B) across three scales (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); whether this trend continues at 70B+ parameters warrants investigation.\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CM3VF9xAS5u6jvgZuusevb`</summary>

```text
36	% === Abstract ===
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
39	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using 15 model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
40	Under these features, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations across five families, 1B--14B; four of five families independently yield $\Kempirical{=}2$, stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv.
41	A fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base classifier cannot break $\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text but extends it to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, establishing a two-tier ceiling: model-free features set a domain-dependent floor while neural classifiers recover additional depth signal on structurally rich domains.
42	A single generation pass renders synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\emph{one-wash laundering}).
43	Largely generator-independent features enable 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B and 96--100\% mean AUC retention at 7B across tested continuation-mode generators, though boundary-level transfer remains fragile; cross-scorer validation confirms $\Kempirical$ robustness for non-borderline configurations (20/22).
44	As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary $>$ graduated filtering for web text, though with limited effect sizes (0.76\,pp MMLU).
45	\end{abstract}
```

</details>

    "oldString": "Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using 15 model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nUnder these features, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations across five families, 1B--14B; four of five families independently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv.\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base classifier cannot break $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text but extends it to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, establishing a two-tier ceiling: model-free features set a domain-dependent floor while neural classifiers recover additional depth signal on structurally rich domains.\nA single generation pass renders synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\\emph{one-wash laundering}).\nLargely generator-independent features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B and 96--100\\% mean AUC retention at 7B across tested continuation-mode generators, though boundary-level transfer remains fragile; cross-scorer validation confirms $\\Kempirical$ robustness for non-borderline configurations (20/22).\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary $>$ graduated filtering for web text, though with limited effect sizes (0.76\\,pp MMLU).",
    "newString": "Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ for web text (8/9 configurations, four of five families, 1B--14B; $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia); a fine-tuned DeBERTa classifier cannot break this ceiling on web text but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv.\nA single generation pass suffices to render synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\\emph{one-wash laundering}); features transfer across generators (80--92\\% accuracy) but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ is fragile.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.",
    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using 15 model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nUnder these features, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations across five families, 1B--14B; four of five families independently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv.\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base classifier cannot break $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text but extends it to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, establishing a two-tier ceiling: model-free features set a domain-dependent floor while neural classifiers recover additional depth signal on structurally rich domains.\nA single generation pass renders synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\\emph{one-wash laundering}).\nLargely generator-independent features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B and 96--100\\% mean AUC retention at 7B across tested continuation-mode generators, though boundary-level transfer remains fragile; cross-scorer validation confirms $\\Kempirical$ robustness for non-borderline configurations (20/22).\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary $>$ graduated filtering for web text, though with limited effect sizes (0.76\\,pp MMLU).\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 9-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (both Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

8:\captionof{table}{Cross-scorer validation on Qwen-generated text. Alternative scorers (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B) preserve feature ranking ($\rho > 0.90$) and rewrite-mode $\Kempirical = 2$, but reduce continuation-mode $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1.}
9:\label{tab:crossscorer}
16:[Omitted long matching line]
17:\label{tab:anova_crossscorer}
48:\caption{Retrain-chain pairwise $\auc$ with 5-fold CV 95\% CIs. Both models yield $\Kempirical{=}7$; all CI lower bounds $> \theta{=}0.60$. Retrain-chain CIs use fold-level estimates (Section~\ref{sec:retrain_ci_note}).}
49:\label{tab:retrain_ci}
80:\caption{$\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$ across conditions. The \textbf{domain ordering} (arXiv $>$ web $\geq$ cross-scorer) and \textbf{bounded range} ($\Kempirical{\leq}4$) are preserved across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.70]$. The headline result (web $\Kempirical{=}2$, arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$) holds for $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$.}
81:\label{tab:theta_sensitivity}
106:\caption{$\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$ across all model $\times$ domain configurations (three panels: C4, arXiv, Wikipedia). The domain ordering (Wikipedia $\Kempirical{\leq}1$ $<$ C4 $\Kempirical{\leq}2$ $<$ arXiv $\Kempirical{\leq}3$) is preserved across the full $\theta \in [0.55, 0.70]$ range. Within each domain, inter-model variation is small relative to inter-domain differences. Source domain rather than model identity governs $\Kempirical$.}
121:\label{tab:pairwise_significance}
132:\caption{MMLU and HellaSwag by contamination ratio and filtering strategy (5 seeds, mean $\pm$ std). Base model MMLU: 0.6018. Binary filtering retains a significant advantage on HellaSwag across all ratios ($p \leq 0.004$, Holm-Bonferroni corrected).}
133:\label{tab:dose_response}
162:\caption{Pythia-1.4B filtering replication (3 seeds, mean $\pm$ std). Strategy ordering matches Qwen (binary $>$ no-filter $>$ graduated on MMLU).}
163:\label{tab:pythia_filtering}
187:\caption{1v2 $\auc$ by feature subset size (Pythia-1.4B). $\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\auc > \theta{=}0.60$) is robust at $\geq$8 features.}
188:\label{tab:feature_subset}
213:\caption{15D vs.\ 14D (self\_BLEU removed) 1v2 $\auc$ with bootstrap 95\% CIs. Removing self\_BLEU does not change $\Kempirical$ for any configuration. Maximum $|\Delta\auc| = 0.0031$, within bootstrap CI width (${\sim}0.018$).}
214:\label{tab:selfbleu_ablation}
242:\caption{Wikipedia pairwise $\auc$ across 6 models. $^\dagger$Qwen rows use same-family scorer (Qwen-1.5B); cross-family scoring (Pythia-1.4B) reduces Qwen-7B to $\Kempirical{=}1$ ($\Delta = -2.4$\,pp), resolving the apparent family split.}
243:\label{tab:wiki_domain}
278:\caption{Wikipedia $\Kempirical$: DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ OBD-LightGBM. DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ from 1 to 3 for 4/5 models; OLMo-1B remains at $\Kempirical{=}1$ under both classifiers. OBD values use cross-family scoring (Table~\ref{tab:wiki_domain}).}
279:\label{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}
304:\caption{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ OBD on arXiv (Qwen-1.5B). DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ from 3 to 4.}
305:\label{tab:deberta_arxiv}
326:\caption{DeBERTa-v3-base on C4 at 1.5B across three generation configurations. $\Kempirical{=}2$ is consistent regardless of generation mode.}
327:\label{tab:deberta_1b5_configs}
349:\caption{DeBERTa cross-model transfer on Wikipedia. Each row is a training model; columns show $\Kempirical$ and boundary $\auc$ when tested on a different model's generations. Cross-transfer drops $\Kempirical$ by ${\sim}1$ level; all X$\to$OLMo pairs yield $\Kempirical{=}1$.}
350:\label{tab:wiki_deberta_transfer}
382:\caption{Bootstrap 95\% CI for arXiv cross-scorer (Pythia-1.4B) 2v3 boundary $\auc$ (10{,}000 resamples). Point estimates classify 3/4 generators as $\Kempirical{=}3$; strict CI lower-bound criterion confirms 2/4.}
383:\label{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}
414:\caption{Retrain-chain AUC decay across 7 generation pairs (Qwen2.5-1.5B LoRA). Unlike prompt-chain (sharp cliff at 0v1$\to$1v2), retrain-chain AUC decays gradually from 0.984 to 0.641, with all pairs above $\theta{=}0.60$. Error bars: 95\% CI from 5-fold CV. Dashed line: $\theta{=}0.60$; extrapolated 7v8 $\approx 0.57$ (below $\theta$), suggesting $\Kempirical{=}7$ is near the ceiling.}
438:\caption{arXiv pairwise $\auc$ with and without LaTeX markup (Qwen-1.5B continuation). Removing formatting reduces 2v3 by ${\sim}$1.8\,pp but $\Kempirical$ remains at 3 (CI lower $> \theta$). LaTeX stripping fraction increases with depth (0.6\% at depth 0, 4.6\% at depth 5), deeper generations therefore produce proportionally more formatting tokens.}
439:\label{tab:latex_ablation}
466:\caption{Subsampling power analysis for $\Kempirical{=}2$ determination (C4, Qwen-1.5B-base, 10 resamples per $n$). Detection probability: fraction of resamples where 1v2 $\auc > \theta{=}0.60$.}
467:\label{tab:power_curve}
485:\caption{$\Kempirical{=}2$ detection rate (left axis, blue) and mean 1v2 $\auc$ (right axis, green) as a function of sample size $n$. Detection rate reaches 100\% at $n{=}2{,}000$; the 80\% power threshold (dotted purple) is crossed between $n{=}1{,}000$ and $n{=}2{,}000$. Error bars: $\pm$1 std over 10 resamples. The dashed red line marks $\theta{=}0.60$.}
502:\caption{Feature group ablation heatmap for 1\,vs.\,2 classification AUC. \emph{Left}: single-group performance. \emph{Right}: leave-one-out performance. Surprisal (base) features are the dominant signal on arXiv (single AUC $= 0.809$) but provide only weak marginal signal on C4 and Wikipedia.}
509:\caption{Feature group ablation for 1\,vs.\,2 classification (AUC). \textbf{Single}: using only features from one group. \textbf{Leave-one-out}: removing one group from the full 15D feature set. $\Delta$ denotes the AUC drop from the full model. Classifier: plain LightGBM (\texttt{n\_estimators}$=$200, \texttt{max\_depth}$=$6), distinct from the OBD-LightGBM pipeline; AUC values are not directly comparable to Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}.}
510:\label{tab:feature_ablation}
543:\caption{McNemar test $p$-values comparing OBD-LightGBM against baseline classifiers on the depth-1 vs.\ depth-2 binary classification task (5-fold GroupKFold, out-of-fold predictions). Classifier accuracy shown in parentheses. Significant results ($p < 0.05$) are \textbf{bold}; $^{*}p<0.05$, $^{**}p<0.01$, $^{***}p<0.001$.}
544:\label{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}
586:\caption{Type-Token Ratio (TTR) and vocabulary entropy across three domains and generation depths.
591:\label{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}
632:\caption{Leave-One-Family-Out (LOFO) cross-validation of $\theta$ across three domains. Each fold holds out one architecture family, derives $\hat{\theta}$ as the midpoint between $\min(\text{AUC}_{1\text{v}2})$ and $\max(\text{AUC}_{2\text{v}3})$ over the remaining families, and predicts $K^*$ for held-out models. C4 (primary): 8/9 agreement (89\%). arXiv: 6/6 (100\%). Wikipedia: 1/6 (17\%, see text).}
633:\label{tab:theta_lofo}
677:\caption{Code domain (Python, CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet}) pairwise AUC for Qwen2.5-1.5B. $\Kempirical{=}2$: 1v2 exceeds $\theta{=}0.60$; 2v3 drops to chance. JSD ratio (1v2/2v3) $= 11.7\times$, comparable to C4's cliff structure.}
678:\label{tab:code_domain_qwen}
697:\caption{Code domain (Python, CodeSearchNet) cross-model pairwise AUC with 5-fold cross-validated 95\% CIs across five models (four architecture families). Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B-v0.3 yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (4/5); Pythia-1.4B yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ (capacity-limited). 757:\label{tab:borderline_features}
777:\caption{Cohen's $d$ (depth-1 vs.\ depth-2) for all 15 features across six C4 models. \textbf{Bold}: largest $|d|$ in each row. 778:\label{tab:cohens_d_full}
815:\caption{7B cross-model transfer: mean pairwise $\auc$ (top) and 0v1 $\auc$ (bottom). Diagonal: within-model. Mean retention 96--100\%; 0v1 transfer $>0.89$ across all pairs. 1v2 boundary is fragile: 4/6 off-diagonal 1v2 pairs fall below $\theta$.}

There's one `\captionof{table}` at line 8, which creates a table without a `table` environment. This can cause numbering issues because `\captionof{table}` increments the table counter but doesn't create a float. If it's inside a `minipage`, the numbering should be fine, but the position in the PDF might cause confusion.

Also, in the main body (experiments_c1.tex), there are several `\captionof{table}` uses:
- tab:neural_probe_crossmodel (line 111)


- tab:deberta_comparison (line 137)
- tab:domain_kstar (line 185)

These are all inline tables using `minipage` + `\captionof{table}`. Since LaTeX numbers tables sequentially regardless of whether they're floats or inline, the numbering should be continuous. The real question is whether the compiled PDF actually shows a gap in the table numbers. I could check the appendix pages in the preview, but that would take time. Instead, let me try compiling and grepping the aux file to see what table numbers are actually being generated.

### Assistant

_source line 13173 · 2026-05-20T04:51:34.445Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I see the issue. The `\captionof{table}` at line 8 is not inside a `table` environment, so LaTeX assigns the table number at the point where it encounters this in the source code. But because of float reordering, the counter might be at 23 by the time LaTeX processes it (because floats are placed lazily and the counter is already advanced by pending floats).

The fix is to convert the `\captionof{table}` + minipage into a proper `table[htbp]` environment. 

Now I'm wrapping the minipage and caption in a proper table environment with placement options to ensure the numbering stays consistent.
```

</details>

```text
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Cross-model transfer heatmap: classifier trained on model A, tested on model B.
3	
4	Input:  artifacts/*transfer*.json, results_*/*transfer*.json, or embedded paper data
5	Output: artifacts/figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf
6	Deps:   matplotlib, numpy
7	"""
8	
9	import argparse
10	import json
11	import matplotlib
12	matplotlib.use("Agg")
13	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
14	import numpy as np
15	from pathlib import Path
16	
17	TABLEAU_COLORS = [
18	    "#4e79a7", "#e15759", "#76b7b2", "#59a14f",
19	    "#b07aa1", "#ff9da7", "#9c755f", "#bab0ac",
20	    "#edc948", "#f28e2b",
21	]
22	
23	EMBEDDED_TRANSFER = {
24	    "pair": "1v2",
25	    "description": "Cross-model transfer AUC (1v2) from paper exp-013",
26	    "models": ["Qwen-rw", "Qwen-cont", "Pythia", "OLMo"],
27	    "matrix": [
28	        [0.6955, 0.6194, 0.5863, 0.5791],
29	        [0.6081, 0.6112, 0.5556, 0.5493],
30	        [0.5917, 0.5632, 0.6539, 0.5988],
31	        [0.5836, 0.5514, 0.5901, 0.6145],
32	    ],
33	}
34	
35	
36	def discover_transfer_files(root):
37	    """Find transfer matrix JSON files."""
38	    patterns = [
39	        "artifacts/*transfer*.json",
40	        "artifacts/*cross_scorer*.json",
41	        "results_*/*transfer*.json",
42	        "results/*transfer*.json",
43	    ]
44	    files = set()
45	    for pat in patterns:
46	        files.update(root.glob(pat))
47	    return sorted(files)
48	
49	
50	def load_transfer_data(json_path):
51	    """Load transfer matrix from JSON."""
52	    with open(json_path) as f:
53	        raw = json.load(f)
54	    if "matrix" in raw and "models" in raw:
55	        return raw["models"], np.array(raw["matrix"]), raw.get("pair", "1v2")
56	    if "transfer_matrix" in raw:
57	        tm = raw["transfer_matrix"]
58	        models = list(tm.keys())
59	        matrix = np.array([[tm[r].get(c, float("nan")) for c in models] for r in models])
60	        return models, matrix, raw.get("pair", "1v2")
61	    return None, None, None
62	
63	
64	def parse_args():
65	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Plot cross-model transfer heatmap")
66	    parser.add_argument("--data-dir", type=Path,
67	                        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent,
68	                        help="Root directory to scan for transfer data")
69	    parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path, default=None,
70	                        help="Output PDF path")
71	    parser.add_argument("--use-embedded", action="store_true",
72	                        help="Force use of embedded paper data")
73	    return parser.parse_args()
74	
75	
76	def main():
77	    args = parse_args()
78	    root = args.data_dir
79	
80	    models, matrix, pair = None, None, None
81	    if not args.use_embedded:
82	        transfer_files = discover_transfer_files(root)
83	        for fp in transfer_files:
84	            models, matrix, pair = load_transfer_data(fp)
85	            if models is not None:
86	                print(f"Loaded transfer data from {fp}")
87	                break
88	
89	    if models is None:
90	        print("No transfer JSON found on disk — using embedded paper data")
91	        models = EMBEDDED_TRANSFER["models"]
92	        matrix = np.array(EMBEDDED_TRANSFER["matrix"])
93	        pair = EMBEDDED_TRANSFER["pair"]
94	
95	    n = len(models)
96	
97	    plt.rcParams.update({
98	        "font.family": "serif",
99	        "font.size": 11,
100	        "axes.linewidth": 0.8,
101	        "xtick.major.width": 0.8,
102	        "ytick.major.width": 0.8,
103	    })
104	
105	    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(5.5, 4.5), constrained_layout=True)
106	
107	    cmap = plt.cm.RdYlGn
108	    vmin, vmax = 0.50, max(0.75, np.nanmax(matrix) + 0.02)
109	    im = ax.imshow(matrix, cmap=cmap, vmin=vmin, vmax=vmax, aspect="equal")
110	
111	    for i in range(n):
112	        for j in range(n):
113	            val = matrix[i, j]
114	            if np.isnan(val):
115	                continue
116	            color = "white" if val > (vmin + vmax) / 2 else "black"
117	            weight = "bold" if i == j else "normal"
118	            ax.text(j, i, f"{val:.3f}", ha="center", va="center",
119	                    fontsize=10, color=color, fontweight=weight)
120	
121	    for i in range(n):
122	        rect = plt.Rectangle((i - 0.5, i - 0.5), 1, 1, linewidth=2.0,
123	                              edgecolor="#333333", facecolor="none", zorder=5)
124	        ax.add_patch(rect)
125	
126	    ax.set_xticks(range(n))
127	    ax.set_xticklabels(models, fontsize=10)
128	    ax.set_yticks(range(n))
129	    ax.set_yticklabels(models, fontsize=10)
130	    ax.set_xlabel("Test model", fontsize=11)
131	    ax.set_ylabel("Train model", fontsize=11)
132	    ax.tick_params(direction="in", length=0)
133	
134	    cbar = fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, shrink=0.82, pad=0.02)
135	    cbar.set_label(f"AUC ({pair})", fontsize=10)
136	    cbar.ax.tick_params(labelsize=9)
137	
138	    if args.output:
139	        out_path = args.output
140	    else:
141	        out_dir = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "figures"
142	        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
143	        out_path = out_dir / "fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf"
144	
145	    out_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
146	    fig.savefig(out_path, dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
147	    png_path = out_path.with_suffix(".png")
148	    fig.savefig(png_path, dpi=300, bbox_inches="tight")
149	    plt.close(fig)
150	
151	    pdf_size = out_path.stat().st_size
152	    png_size = png_path.stat().st_size
153	    print(f"Models: {models}")
154	    print(f"PDF: {pdf_size/1024:.1f} KB | PNG: {png_size/1024:.1f} KB")
155	    assert pdf_size > 3 * 1024, f"PDF too small: {pdf_size}"
156	    print("OK")
157	
158	
159	if __name__ == "__main__":
160	    main()
161

patterns:\n        files.update(root.glob(pat))\n    return sorted(files)\n\n\ndef load_transfer_data(json_path):\n    \"\"\"Load transfer matrix from JSON.\"\"\"\n    with open(json_path) as f:\n        raw = json.load(f)\n    if \"matrix\" in raw and \"models\" in raw:\n        return raw[\"models\"], np.array(raw[\"matrix\"]), raw.get(\"pair\", \"1v2\")\n    if \"transfer_matrix\" in raw:\n parser.add_argument(\"--data-dir\", type=Path,\n                        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent,\n                        help=\"Root directory to scan for transfer data\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--output\", type=Path, default=None,\n                        help=\"Output PDF path\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--use-embedded\", action=\"store_true\",\n plt.rcParams.update({\n        \"font.family\": \"serif\",\n        \"font.size\": 11,\n        \"axes.linewidth\": 0.8,\n        \"xtick.major.width\": 0.8,\n        \"ytick.major.width\": 0.8,\n    })\n\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(5.5, 4.5), constrained_layout=True)\n\n    cmap = plt.cm.RdYlGn\n    vmin, vmax = 0.50, max(0.75, np.nanmax(matrix) + 0.02)\n    im = ax.imshow(matrix,       color = \"white\" if val > (vmin + vmax) / 2 else \"black\"\n            weight = \"bold\" if i == j else \"normal\"\n            ax.text(j, i, f\"{val:.3f}\", ha=\"center\", va=\"center\",\n edgecolor=\"#333333\", facecolor=\"none\", zorder=5)\n        ax.add_patch(rect)\n\n    ax.set_xticks(range(n))\n    ax.set_xticklabels(models, fontsize=10)\n    ax.set_yticks(range(n))\n    This is a Paper Agent session for "gen-depth-contamination" EMNLP 2026 ARR submission (DDL May 25, 2026). The Director issued Round 17 审稿修复 with 4 priority blocks, with explicit instruction: "严格按以下优先级顺序执行，每完成一个 block 汇报进度。" The session resumed from context compaction with BLOCK 1 partially done (edits made, compile passed, but page boundary not visually verified). All 4 blocks have now been implemented and verified.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - K* (discrimination boundary) = max depth where AUC > θ=0.60
   - Two-tier ceiling framework: OBD (model-free features) K*∈{1,2,3} vs DeBERTa (neural) K*={2,3,4}
   - Eq.1 monotonicity: changed from `max{k≥1: CI_low(AUC_{k-1,k}) > θ}` to `max{k≥1: CI_low(AUC_{j-1,j}) > θ, ∀j≤k}`
   - AUC gap as continuous measure (1v2 minus 2v3: 5.0-10.6pp across configurations)
   - K* is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold θ
   - LOFO = internal consistency check, not external validation
   - EMNLP 2026: strict 8-page main body limit (Limitations/Ethics/References don't count)
   - Paper Agent communicates via paper_agent_post_to_director (NOT main_agent_post_to_director)
   - Colorblind-safe colormaps: viridis replaces RdYlGn for accessibility

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/method.tex`** (MF-1 + MF-2)
     - Eq.1 changed to ∀j≤k formulation with tighter spacing
     - Added monotonicity statement and feature-set conditioning language
     - Current Eq.1 (line 14):
     ```latex
     \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{j\text{-}1,j}) > \theta,\ \forall j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
     ```
     - Post-equation text (lines 18-21):
     ```latex
     This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise; the $\forall j \leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because adjacent-pair AUC monotonically decreases across all nine C4 configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}), consistent with DPI-implied information loss.
     Since 0v1 $\auc > \theta$ in all tested configurations, $\Kempirical \geq 1$; $\Kempirical{=}0$ (undetectable first-generation text) is not observed.
     $\Kempirical$ is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold $\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), though absolute $\Kempirical$ values shift with $\theta$.
     We additionally verify that non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/main.tex`** (Abstract - M-1 + MF-3)
     - Simplified from 7 to 5 sentences, 3 core claims, reduced number stacking
     - Current Abstract (lines 37-44):
     ```latex
     \begin{abstract}
     Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
     We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using model-free statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
     $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text (8/9 configurations, four of five families, 1B--14B; $\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia); a fine-tuned DeBERTa classifier cannot break this ceiling on web text but extends $\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv.
     A single generation pass suffices to render synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\emph{one-wash laundering}); features transfer across generators (80--92\% accuracy) but boundary-level $\Kempirical$ is fragile.
     As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.
     \end{abstract}
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/experiments_c1.tex`** (Section 4 - MF-1 + MF-3 + SF-1)
     - Section 4.1: Added "at θ=0.60", "(5 Qwen + 3 non-Qwen)", AUC gap sentence
     - Section 4.2: Title changed from "Largely Generator-Independent Transfer" to "Cross-Model Transfer and Its Limits"
     - Section 4 summary: Added "at θ=0.60" qualifier
     - Key paragraph (lines 44-47):
     ```latex
     Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:auc_decay} show $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ across all five tested architecture families on C4, with 8/9 individual configurations (5 Qwen + 3 non-Qwen; 1v2 $\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6955; all CI lower bounds $> \theta$; permutation $p < 0.001$); Qwen contributes 5 configurations spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B).
     The 1v2 vs.\ 2v3 AUC gap ranges from 5.0 to 10.6\,pp across configurations, exhibiting a sharp cliff at the boundary: all 2v3 values fall below $\theta$ while 1v2 values remain 1--10\,pp above it.
     Mistral-7B is the sole exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$; 1v2 $\auc = 0.594$; Section~\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).
     Among non-Qwen families: Pythia and OLMo yield clear $\Kempirical{=}2$, LLaMA-3.1-8B is borderline (1v2 $\auc = 0.6123$, CI lower $= 0.603$; 1/5 folds below $\theta$), and Mistral yields $\Kempirical{=}1$.
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/introduction.tex`** (SF-1 + SF-2)
     - Contribution (c) changed from "Practical validation" to "Transfer and consistency check"
     - Added "within continuation mode" and "boundary-level K* is fragile" caveats
     ```latex
     \item \textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\rho > 0.90$; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\Kempirical$ is fragile under cross-model transfer at 7B; as a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary $>$ graduated filtering for C4 web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/discussion.tex`** (SF-2 + SF-6 + MF-1)
     - Added "at θ=0.60" to first K*=2 mention
     - Added discriminability vs filtering utility sentence (SF-2)
     - Added scale extrapolation sentence (SF-6)
     - Current added text at end of JSD paragraph:
     ```latex
     Discriminability ($\Kempirical$) does not automatically translate to filtering utility: on arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering still fails to outperform binary (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
     LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.618 (32B); extrapolation to 70B+ warrants investigation.
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/conclusion.tex`** (SF-1 + page compression)
     - Compressed multiple times to fit within page 8
     - Current state:
     ```latex
     Under prompt-chain recursion at $\theta{=}0.60$, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text (8/9 configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; domain ordering is $\theta$-invariant.
     DeBERTa cannot break $\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 (1.5B--32B) but extends it to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (two-tier ceiling); cross-model transfer retains 80--92\% aggregate accuracy but boundary-level $\Kempirical$ remains fragile.
     Future work: retrain-chain $\Kempirical$, scaling beyond 32B.
     ```

   - **`docs/paper/appendix_extended.tex`** (M-3 table numbering fix)
     - Changed `\captionof{table}` in minipage to proper `\begin{table}[htbp]` for tab:crossscorer
     - This fixed the table numbering discontinuity (Table 23 was appearing between Tables 7 and 8)

   - **`artifacts/scripts/transfer_heatmap.py`** (R13-M5)
     - Changed `cmap = plt.cm.RdYlGn` to `cmap = plt.cm.viridis` on line 107
     - Regenerated `docs/paper/figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf` with `--use-embedded` flag

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Eq.1 overfull hbox (19pt → 5pt)**: The new ∀j≤k constraint made the equation too wide. Fixed by adding `\!` negative thin spaces around `\geq` and `\leq`, using `\bigl\{...\bigr\}` instead of `\{...\}`, and `\text{-}` for the hyphen.

Fixed by iteratively compressing: (1) merged DeBERTa + transfer sentences with semicolon, (2) shortened "Future work" to a telegraphic format, (3) removed domain ordering details from the first sentence, (4) compressed Discussion SF-2/SF-6 sentences.

features transfer across generators (80--92\\% accuracy) but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ is fragile.\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 9-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (both Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 96
      }

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nUnder prompt-chain recursion at $\\theta{=}0.60$, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text (8/9 configurations), $\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; domain ordering is $\\theta$-invariant.\nDeBERTa cannot break $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 (1.5B--32B) but extends it to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (two-tier ceiling); cross-model transfer retains 80--92\\% aggregate accuracy but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ remains fragile.\nFuture work: retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$, scaling beyond 32B.\n",

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nEven frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \\geq 2$ ranges 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix).\nSince all main results use prompt-chain (fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain), $\\Kempirical{=}2$ establishes a detection floor; retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) represents the upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay, domain dependence, and the two-tier ceiling.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$), while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); the retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) extends $\\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nThe domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), and the classifier gap scales accordingly: DeBERTa recovers $+0$ on C4, $+1$ on Wikipedia, and $+1$ on arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), with the zero gap on C4 (stable across 1.5B--32B) indicating fundamental information loss rather than classifier limitation.\nDiscriminability ($\\Kempirical$) does not automatically translate to filtering utility: on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering still fails to outperform binary (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\nLightGBM 1v2 AUC declines from 0.626 (1.5B) to 0.618 (32B); extrapolation to 70B+ warrants investigation.\n\n",
        "numLines": 28,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 28
      }
    },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VJsQy5zWMEZhaq4Br7SiUd`</summary>

```text
14	  \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{j\text{-}1,j}) > \theta,\ \forall j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
15	  \label{eq:kstar}
16	\end{equation}
17	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
18	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise; the $\forall j \leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because adjacent-pair AUC monotonically decreases across all nine C4 configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}), consistent with DPI-implied information loss.
19	Since 0v1 $\auc > \theta$ in all tested configurations, $\Kempirical \geq 1$; $\Kempirical{=}0$ (undetectable first-generation text) is not observed.
20	$\Kempirical$ is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold $\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), though absolute $\Kempirical$ values shift with $\theta$.
21	We additionally verify that non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
22	
23	\paragraph{Threshold choice.}
24	We set $\theta{=}0.60$ as a post-hoc operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
25	A 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\,0.55$ and $p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons, placing $\theta{=}0.60$ well above the empirical null.
26	$\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.65]$ for all but two marginal configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}): 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp discontinuity.
27	Only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift: $\theta{=}0.55$ yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\theta{=}0.70$ yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ across all configurations.
28	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates a finite $\\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI guarantees existence but does not bound $\\Ktheory$, so the actual saturation depth must be measured empirically.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\\Kempirical$):\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{j\\text{-}1,j}) > \\theta,\\ \\forall j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).\nThis criterion ensures that $\\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability rather than sampling noise; the $\\forall j \\leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because adjacent-pair AUC monotonically decreases across all nine C4 configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}), consistent with DPI-implied information loss.\nSince 0v1 $\\auc > \\theta$ in all tested configurations, $\\Kempirical \\geq 1$; $\\Kempirical{=}0$ (undetectable first-generation text) is not observed.\n$\\Kempirical$ is an operational quantity jointly conditioned on the feature set and threshold $\\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), though absolute $\\Kempirical$ values shift with $\\theta$.\nWe additionally verify that non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold choice.}\nWe set $\\theta{=}0.60$ as a post-hoc operational threshold, chosen at the empirical discontinuity in the AUC distribution (Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nA 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ and $p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons, placing $\\theta{=}0.60$ well above the empirical null.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.55, 0.65]$ for all but two marginal configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}): 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity.\nOnly at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift: $\\theta{=}0.55$ yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$ yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all configurations.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\nA leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation on C4 provides an internal consistency check (not external validation): each of five folds derives $\\hat{\\theta}$ from the gap midpoint between $\\min(\\auc_{1v2})$ and $\\max(\\auc_{2v3})$ over the remaining families, yielding $\\hat{\\theta} \\in [0.5725, 0.6004]$; $\\theta{=}0.60$ falls within this range, and 8/9 held-out predictions match the original $\\Kempirical$ (the single mismatch is the borderline rewrite-mode configuration; Table~\\ref{tab:theta_lofo}).\nArXiv corroborates with 6/6 LOFO agreement; Wikipedia is underdetermined because most models produce near-chance 1v2 AUC, invalidating the gap-based derivation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU is computed within each CV fold using only same-fold, same-depth reference samples to prevent leakage; it ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations; bootstrap CIs in Table~\\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}); in deployment where depth labels are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted with minimal performance loss.\nHand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer without retraining and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate nine configurations spanning five architecture families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation modes), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base, continuation), Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-14B, Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B (all continuation except Qwen-rw).\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4~\\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nMistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n",

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex:5:Iterative training on model outputs causes tail-distribution degradation~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, dohmatob2024model, dohmatob2025strong}, diversity contraction~\cite{guo2024curious, padmakumar2024writing, briesch2023selfconsuming}, and predictable convergence~\cite{bertrand2024stability, schaeffer2025position, kazdan2025collapse, gerstgrasser2024model, shi2025collapse}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-6-\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} propose surplexity, a single surprisal-based collapse indicator; our 15-dimensional feature set captures richer distributional structure and enables cross-model transfer.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-7-\citet{amin2025escaping} show that weak-data curation can mitigate collapse, connecting to our filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-8-Our prompt-chain operates in the replace regime~\cite{kazdan2025collapse}; $\Kempirical = 2$ is therefore a conservative lower bound.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-9-This literature establishes convergence but provides no per-text depth measurement, does not predict domain dependence of saturation (Wikipedia depth 1 vs.\ arXiv depth 3), and does not characterize the prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain gap ($\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\ $7$).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-10-
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-11-\paragraph{Beyond binary detection.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-12-Binary detectors~\cite{gehrmann2019gltr, mitchell2023detectgpt, bao2024fast, yang2024dnagpt, hans2024binoculars, verma2024ghostbuster, basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} achieve strong human-vs.-machine separation but produce no depth estimate; surveys~\cite{wu2023survey, tang2024science} consolidate this landscape but frame detection as binary.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/related_work.tex-13-Recent work extends beyond binary labels to collaboration degree~\cite{cheng2025beyond}, contamination hierarchies~\cite{mehta2025beyond}, and multigenerator benchmarks~\cite{wang2024semeval}; robustness concerns arise under paraphrasing~\cite{sadasivan2023detect, krishna2023paraphrasing} and cross-mode transfer~\cite{hao2025rewrite}; CoDeC~\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses contamination via membership inference.

  "sessionId": "952e4180-a1dd-4838-893f-04003731e881",
  "content": "[服务器重启后首条消息] 当前日期: 2026-05-20。服务器刚重启，项目状态可能已更新。不要依赖对话历史中的状态信息，以 registry 当前值为准。\n\n**用户消息：** [Director 指令] [Patch] 批量整合新实验结果，目标 EMNLP 2026。实验已跑完，论文写作与剩余 2 个实验并行推进。\n\n**需要写入的新结果（6 项）：**\n\n1. **Gemma-2-2B K*=1**（negative）— C4 域 1v2 AUC=0.5981, CI=[0.5882,0.6076]。记为 competence-limited，与 Mistral K*=1 并列。主结果表（Table 1 或对应表格）加 Gemma 行，标 K*=1。\n\n2. **Code DeBERTa K*=2**（success）— DeBERTa=OBD=2，Code 域 representation-independent ceiling 确认。写入 DeBERTa 结果表/段落。2v3=0.5933 CI upper=0.6018 微跨 θ，需注明。\n\n3. **Changepoint θ-free K* 验证**（success）— 35 实验中 33 个（94.6%）changepoint K* 与 θ=0.60 K* 差距 ≤1，CP 方法始终更保守（K*_CP ≤ K*_θ），域阶梯保持。消除 \"θ 怎么选\" 的审稿 concern。新增一段或一个表。\n\n4. **Multi-source C4 K*=2**（success）— 混合 3 个生成器（Qwen/Pythia/OLMo），K*=2 三重确认（统一 scorer 0.7105、纯词汇 0.6830、原始混合 0.7007）。Generator label 无增益（+0.008 AUC）。文献 21 篇中 0 篇做过，最强 novelty 点。\n\n5. **Multi-source arXiv K*=3**（success）— 3 生成器（Qwen/Pythia/LLaMA-8B）混合，K*=3 与单源一致，域阶梯 C4(2)&lt;arXiv(3) 在多源下不变。arXiv depth 2→3 区分依赖 surprisal 特征。\n\n6. **arXiv DeBERTa K*=4**（success）— DeBERTa > OBD(K*=3)，与 Wiki DeBERTa K*=3 > OBD≤2 同 pattern。高复杂度域 neural classifier 能看到更深层信号。\n\n**Framing 调整（D089）：**\n- \"K*=2 is architecture-independent on C4\" → \"domain ceiling K*=2, achievable by 7/9 conditions; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B are competence-limited at K*=1\"\n- 强调 two-tier framework（domain ceiling + model competence）的实证基础：低复杂度域 DeBERTa=OBD（C4, Code），高复杂度域 DeBERTa>OBD（Wiki, arXiv）\n- Multi-source 作为独特贡献重点突出\n\n**还在跑的实验（后续 Patch）：**\n- 7b cross-transfer（~1-2h 完成）\n- OLMo retrain chain v2（~2-4h 完成）\n完成后我会再派 Patch。\n\n**引用这次不用验证。** 正文 ≤8 页硬约束。"
}

_source line 13293 · 2026-05-20T09:52:00.309Z_

[服务器重启后首条消息] 当前日期: 2026-05-20。服务器刚重启，项目状态可能已更新。不要依赖对话历史中的状态信息，以 registry 当前值为准。

**用户消息：** [Director 指令] [Patch] 批量整合新实验结果，目标 EMNLP 2026。实验已跑完，论文写作与剩余 2 个实验并行推进。

**需要写入的新结果（6 项）：**

1. **Gemma-2-2B K*=1**（negative）— C4 域 1v2 AUC=0.5981, CI=[0.5882,0.6076]。记为 competence-limited，与 Mistral K*=1 并列。主结果表（Table 1 或对应表格）加 Gemma 行，标 K*=1。

2. **Code DeBERTa K*=2**（success）— DeBERTa=OBD=2，Code 域 representation-independent ceiling 确认。写入 DeBERTa 结果表/段落。2v3=0.5933 CI upper=0.6018 微跨 θ，需注明。

3. **Changepoint θ-free K* 验证**（success）— 35 实验中 33 个（94.6%）changepoint K* 与 θ=0.60 K* 差距 ≤1，CP 方法始终更保守（K*_CP ≤ K*_θ），域阶梯保持。消除 "θ 怎么选" 的审稿 concern。新增一段或一个表。

4. **Multi-source C4 K*=2**（success）— 混合 3 个生成器（Qwen/Pythia/OLMo），K*=2 三重确认（统一 scorer 0.7105、纯词汇 0.6830、原始混合 0.7007）。Generator label 无增益（+0.008 AUC）。文献 21 篇中 0 篇做过，最强 novelty 点。

5. **Multi-source arXiv K*=3**（success）— 3 生成器（Qwen/Pythia/LLaMA-8B）混合，K*=3 与单源一致，域阶梯 C4(2)&lt;arXiv(3) 在多源下不变。arXiv depth 2→3 区分依赖 surprisal 特征。

6. **arXiv DeBERTa K*=4**（success）— DeBERTa > OBD(K*=3)，与 Wiki DeBERTa K*=3 > OBD≤2 同 pattern。高复杂度域 neural classifier 能看到更深层信号。

**Framing 调整（D089）：**
- "K*=2 is architecture-independent on C4" → "domain ceiling K*=2, achievable by 7/9 conditions; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B are competence-limited at K*=1"
- 强调 two-tier framework（domain ceiling + model competence）的实证基础：低复杂度域 DeBERTa=OBD（C4, Code），高复杂度域 DeBERTa>OBD（Wiki, arXiv）
- Multi-source 作为独特贡献重点突出

**还在跑的实验（后续 Patch）：**
- 7b cross-transfer（~1-2h 完成）
- OLMo retrain chain v2（~2-4h 完成）
完成后我会再派 Patch。

**引用这次不用验证。** 正文 ≤8 页硬约束。

Graduated filtering = no_filter at every ratio. Effect size of binary advantage increases with contamination ratio (HellaSwag delta: 10%=+0.38pp, 30%=+0.61pp, 50%=+0.51pp). Original C3 hypothesis (graduated > binary) rejected.\", \"gpu_hours_total\": 9.9}, \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp_009_dose_50pct_v3\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-17T00:13:08.251Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-17T10:23:52.356Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"50% synthetic injection 下 graduated filtering 应展现最大优势 >=2pp MMLU（v3: fix OOM, auto batch + offline）。半数数据为合成时 binary 激进丢弃导致数据饥饿。\", \"parent\": \"exp_009_dose_50pct_v2\", \"metrics\": {\"seeds\": 5, \"strategies\": 3, \"total_runs\": 15, \"mean_std\": {\"no_filter\": {\"mmlu\": \"0.6063±0.0013\", \"hellaswag\": \"0.6680±0.0005\"}, \"binary_ours\": {\"mmlu\": \"0.6088±0.0011\", \"hellaswag\": \"0.6731±0.0004\"}, \"graduated\": {\"mmlu\": \"0.6062±0.0009\", \"hellaswag\": \"0.6679±0.0002\"}}, \"paired_ttest\": {\"binary_vs_nofilter\": {\"delta_mmlu\": 0.0025, \"p_mmlu\": 0.0377, \"delta_hellaswag\": 0.0051, \"p_hellaswag\": 2e-05}, \"graduated_vs_binary\": {\"delta_mmlu\": -0.0026, \"p_mmlu\": 0.0257, \"delta_hellaswag\": -0.0052, \"p_hellaswag\": 3e-05}, \"graduated_vs_nofilter\": {\"delta_mmlu\": -0.0001, \"p_mmlu\": 0.89, \"delta_hellaswag\": -0.0001, \"p_hellaswag\": 0.706}}, \"key_finding\": \"Binary significantly best (HellaSwag p<0.001).
